## 0. Role and ground rules

You are working on Phantom Scan (Thaer Bayyari, lead; supervisor Dr. Ahmad Al-Nawasrah; stakeholder JODDB). Wave 0–2 are done and the owner has now made his Gate 2 decisions (section 2). This prompt covers Wave 3 plus the preparation needed so that the new recordings can be analysed the day they exist.

Non-negotiable rules (unchanged from earlier waves):

1. **Hardware truth.** Two-node fused is the primary mode. One Rx antenna per node. No AoA, no pose, no body shape. Only 1-D on-axis energy-balance position. Anything else must carry the existing caveat pattern (`getLivePositionCaveat`, `canPresentLiveTargetPosition`, `canRenderConfidentHuman`).
2. **F1 and F2 stay intact.** AI/ML never writes the authoritative verdict. The dashboard never writes upstream to nodes, serial or firmware. Re-run `tests/test_invariants.py::test_f1_*`, `test_f2_*`, and the AI-firewall tests explicitly after every item that touches detection, gating, AI integration, display, ingest or protocol.
3. **Honesty.** Unmeasured numbers carry `[TO BE MEASURED]`. Claims you did not verify in this session carry `[UNVERIFIED]`. Re-verify every claim from earlier reports against the current files before acting on it. Negative results ("could not reproduce", "no effect") are valid outcomes; do not invent a fix.
4. **No safety constant changes.** Do not change the presence threshold (0.6), room tier thresholds (4/7/14), or the fallback 0.05. PS-EVAL-010 and PS-EVAL-013 stay with the owner. Flag, do not guess.
5. **Do not touch the running dashboard or the user's live data.** All server tests use the isolated port 5099 guard. Never bind 5000/5005 in tests. Never write into `data/sessions/` except through the verified restore procedure. Before and after any test run, confirm `data/sessions/_index.json` sha256 prefix is still `0261130fef4cfed9` and the file count is unchanged (730 plus the 5 quarantined in `.tmp/quarantine-sessions-2026-11-03/`). If it differs, stop and report.
6. **No flashing, no serial writes.** Firmware items are host-tested only. Nothing in this wave may open a serial port for writing.
7. **One item at a time.** Fix, test, full suite green, report that item, then the next. Stop on the first red.
8. **Both failure directions.** Every item that can change what is shown must be checked against real recordings for under-detection (person present, no marker) and for ghost targets (empty room, marker shown). State which direction each change can affect and show the numbers.
9. **Full suites before and after each item, pasted in full, not paraphrased:** the TypeScript suite, `tsc`, and the Python suite. Baseline from Wave 2: TS 444/444, tsc 0 errors, Python 342 passed + 5 opt-in skips, F1/F2 3/3. Any change in these counts must be explained in the item report.
10. **Quantify tradeoffs** (dB, latency in ms, cycles or bytes of flash/RAM for firmware, % of frames affected). No qualitative-only statements.
11. Commit messages end with the attribution lines required by the session reminder. One commit per item. Do not push.

Work in `reports/fix-2026-11/`. Update `80-issues-ledger`, `89-merge-gate` and `90-test-report` after each item. Add a new `09-WAVE3-addendum.md` for this wave.

## 3. Work items (in this order)

### Item A — PS-FIX-38: stop documenting an unsafe threshold rule

Docs still describe the presence threshold as "mean + k·sigma" derived from a short run. The committed derivation used a 60 s empty recording (mean 0.49, σ 0.027, max 0.58 → 0.6). A 60 s window cannot support a k·σ rule because the real empty-vs-empty drift is larger than within-recording σ.

- `grep` the docs, README, dashboard help text, exported-report templates and code comments for the rule. List every hit with file and line.
- Rewrite each hit to say what was actually done (single short empty recording, value 0.6, provisional) and what real recalibration requires (live empty-room soak ≥ 60 min with entry log, plus a negative control with fan/vibration, using the project's calibration tooling). Mark the false-alarm and detection figures `[TO BE MEASURED]`.
- Add a doc-lint test that fails if the phrase "mean + k" (any spacing/σ/sigma variant) reappears in docs or exported-report templates without the `[TO BE MEASURED]` or "provisional" qualifier in the same paragraph.
- Do not change the threshold value.

### Item B — PS-FIX-37: make the frame-alternation finding reproducible

Background (from the owner's reviewer, exploratory, scratch scripts outside the repo, so `[UNVERIFIED]` until you reproduce it): on all four nodes in session `s6`, correlation between a frame and the frame two later is higher than with its immediate neighbour (N3 lag-2 0.82 vs lag-1 0.72), and RSSI differences are smaller at lag 2 than lag 1. This fits about 2 frames per 30 ms ping. Recomputing features on same-parity substreams did **not** remove the empty-vs-empty drift (AUC stays ≈ 0.93–1.00 for perturb/decor), so interleaving is not the main cause of the between-session drift. It does lower the decor level by about 25–37 % (N3 0.282 → 0.178).

- Add `scripts/presence_eval/frame_parity.py` (read-only, reads `data/sessions/`): per node and session, lag-1/lag-2/lag-3 correlation of demeaned subcarrier vectors, lag-1/lag-2 RSSI difference, inter-frame interval histogram, and the same-parity decor vs all-frames decor.
- Run it on every available multi-node session and write the table to `reports/fix-2026-11/results/frame-parity.md`. If you cannot reproduce the alternation, say so plainly and show what you tried.
- State explicitly in the report: this does not explain the drift; it affects decor level and the choice of lag; it motivates Item G (transmitter field).
- Serial counters: do **not** write code that opens a serial port. Write `docs/serial-counter-capture.md`, a short procedure the owner follows by hand (which firmware counters to read, how to paste the log), plus a parser `scripts/presence_eval/parse_serial_counters.py` that reads a pasted text file. This keeps F2 intact.

### Item C — PS-FIX-40: resampling aliases the ping cadence

Finding from Wave 2: the 50 Hz resample aliases the 33.3 Hz ping cadence to 16.7 Hz. Frames arrive at about 65 fps per node, roughly 2 per ping.

- Locate every resample or fixed-rate assumption in `phantom/dsp.py` and the TS pipeline (grep for 50, `fs`, `resample`, `decimate`, `interp`). List them with file:line.
- Demonstrate the alias with a synthetic test (33.3 Hz impulse train through the current resampler) and with one real session (spectrum before/after). Include the respiration (0.1–0.5 Hz) and cardiac (0.8–2.0 Hz) bands: show whether the alias lands inside either.
- Propose the fix: resample to a rate ≥ 64 Hz (justify the exact choice with the Nyquist margin for 33.3 Hz and its harmonics) or avoid fixed resampling and use timestamp-aware processing. Implement the smallest change that removes the alias. Keep the old path behind a flag only if a test needs it.
- Regression tests: synthetic alias test; band-energy test; existing DSP tests unchanged.
- Check both failure directions: replay the same real empty and walking sessions before/after and report presence-gate output counts. If the numbers move, report by how much and do not touch the threshold.

### Item D — small hardening

Wrap the call to `phyGuard.observe` in `server/lib/udp-server.ts` in try/catch so a guard fault can never drop or delay a frame. Log once per 1000 errors, not per frame. Add a test that injects a throwing guard and proves the frame is still ingested. Run the full TS suite.

### Item E — PS-FIX-16: fail-closed outputs

- Define, in a short design section first, which outputs fail closed (no data, stale data, node missing, rate collapse, PHY mismatch in shadow mode) and what the user sees instead (state chip NO DATA / SETTLING, never EMPTY).
- Implement with the existing caveat pattern. Never present "empty" as a verdict from missing data.
- Replay check on real sessions, both directions: (a) the fail-closed rules must not suppress a real walking segment; (b) they must not produce a marker on an empty or no-data segment. Show counts per session.
- F1/F2/AI-firewall tests re-run explicitly.

### Item F — PS-FIX-14: firmware robustness (host-tested only)

- Bounded calibration retry (today it can retry without bound) and a CSI stall watchdog, in `edge_processing.c` / `csi_collector.c` as appropriate. Verify the current behaviour in the source first; do not rely on this prompt's description.
- Everything new behind a Kconfig option, **default OFF**. No behaviour change in a default build. Prove it: build the host tests with the option off and show the compiled logic is unchanged (diff of symbol list or test outputs).
- Host tests for the pure logic (`edge_dsp_pure.c` style). Report added flash and RAM in bytes and worst-case cycles per frame for the watchdog check, as estimates marked `[UNVERIFIED]` unless measured on target.
- Do not flash. State that it has never run on hardware.

### Item G — PS-FIX-36: header fields for bandwidth, sig_mode, transmitter

Goal: let the host tell the two frame types per ping apart and enforce PHY assumptions later (PS-FIX-13, still not approved).

- Design first: new fields, byte layout in the 32-byte header (or the smallest compatible extension), version negotiation, how old firmware/new server and new firmware/old server behave. Quantify bytes added per frame and per second at 65 fps × 4 nodes.
- Dispatch order: check the longer / more specific magic first. Add the regression test for exactly that bug class, plus tests for old-format frames still parsing identically (byte-for-byte golden frames from real recordings).
- Implement the server parser and firmware pack function with host tests only. **Not flashed.** Shadow-mode PHY guard may read the new fields if present; it must still never drop frames.
- Document the single-owner ingest rule is unchanged (UDP 5005, no shared bind).

### Item H — PS-FIX-12: rate-scaled timing (design note only, then STOP)

Write `reports/fix-2026-11/10-PS-FIX-12-design.md` only. Cover: every time constant that is currently counted in frames and silently assumes a fixed rate (list with file:line), how each would scale with the measured per-node rate, the failure cases when the rate is wrong or changes mid-session (link-state epochs), quantified effect on window lengths in seconds at 11, 41–43 and 65 fps, and the test plan including replay on real sessions in both failure directions. Do not write production code. End with the exact questions the owner must answer at Gate 3.

### Item I — licence hygiene

Re-verify PS-FIX-24 and -26 against current files. Confirm in `06-licences` that `pose_v1` and any MM-Fi-derived weights, data or code are not in the repo's deliverable paths, exported reports, docs or the dashboard bundle. Add a check script that fails if such paths or strings appear in the report templates or the packaged build. Report; do not delete anything without asking.

## 4. Recording-readiness (no new recordings exist yet)

The blocker for detection work is data, not code. Prepare the repo so analysis starts the moment recordings land.

1. **Pre-registration file first.** Create `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md` and commit it **before** any new recording is analysed. It states the success criteria in advance: required AUC lower confidence bound for each candidate feature, the C4 empty-vs-empty gate (empty vs second empty must stay near 0.5; if not, the link state changed and the block is invalid), minimum durations, and what counts as failure. Use numbers derived from the existing results (no candidate passed before); where you cannot justify a number, write `[OWNER TO SET]` instead of picking one.
2. **Recording protocol as data spec.** Describe the folder layout, naming, metadata sidecar fields (block type, start/stop UTC, entry log, link-state notes, fan on/off, person count, node placement) and the LINK CHANGE rule (≥ 6 dB RSSI shift or fps collapse → stop, log, wait 2 min, new block). Blocks: ≥ 60 min empty with entry log; fan alone 5 min; walking 5 min; still 5 min; second empty ≥ 60 min; fan with a person present. All inside one link state.
3. **Ingest validator.** A script that takes a new session folder and refuses (non-zero exit, clear message) when: sidecar missing, block shorter than required (PS-FIX-11 rules), fps outside the expected band, RSSI step ≥ 6 dB inside a block, node missing, or epochs not segmented. It never writes into `data/sessions/`.
4. **One-command analysis.** `scripts/presence_eval/run_campaign.py <folder>` runs epoch segmentation, parity-aware features (Item B), causal self-baseline movement score (repo rule: ≥ 80 % of lookback windows), AUC with block-bootstrap CIs, the C4 gate, and writes a report with every number labelled measured or `[TO BE MEASURED]`. Dry-run it on existing sessions and show it behaves the same as the current repo tooling (same numbers within stated tolerance).
5. Do not change any threshold from the output of this script in this wave.

## 5. Final report (required format)

Write `reports/fix-2026-11/09-WAVE3-addendum.md` and give me, in chat:

1. A table, one row per item A–I plus section 4: ID, status (done / design only / skipped / blocked), commit hash, tests added, **blocking or non-blocking**, failure direction checked (under-detection / ghost / neither) with numbers.
2. The 11-vs-46 answer.
3. The **full, unabridged** output of the TS suite, `tsc`, the Python suite, and the explicit F1/F2/AI-firewall runs, before and after. Not paraphrased.
4. The `_index.json` sha256 prefix and file count before and after.
5. A list of what you could not verify and why, each tagged `[UNVERIFIED]`.
6. The Gate 3 questions for the owner (including those from Item H).
7. Any red flag you found in earlier reports' claims (things that drifted).

Do not push. Do not flash. Do not delete files without asking first.

The runner is built and tested, and I have not run any step in your repository. Nothing is staged and HEAD is still `0e61373`. I'm waiting for you to say the `[OWNER TO SET]` items are final before I run step P.

**Where it is.** `.tmp/commit-steps/run_steps.py` does nothing without `--go`. With `--go` it does the following:
- Checks that `.tmp/pre-wave3-backup` exists and that `MANIFEST.sha256` verifies. It did when I checked a moment ago.
- Refuses to start if anything is already staged.
- Snapshots the working tree, then runs P, O1–O8, M-01…M-08, M-10…M-14 and R in order.
- Before each step it prints the expected stat. After staging it prints `git diff --cached --stat` and commits only if the two are identical.
- On any difference it stops with exit 3, leaves the index staged for you to inspect and commits nothing for that step.
- It never pushes and never touches `data/`, serial ports or the flasher.
- At the end it prints the final proof: every pre-commit file equals its `HEAD` blob, plus `git diff HEAD --stat -- . :!data :!node_modules` and `git status`.

**What I tested.**
- **Dry run on the real repo:** the expected stats parse for all steps.
- **Throwaway clone:** step P staged and committed with identical stats. I then altered O2's expected stat by one line (74 → 75 insertions). The run printed both stats, reported the mismatch, stopped, and left O2 staged but uncommitted.
- **Not tested:** the full end-to-end run with the final proof, because the clone has no uncommitted working tree to compare against. It is untested until the real run.

For P and R, the expected stat is computed on a scratch index at run time, because 88b has no exact figure for them. P currently expects 1 file, +83 lines.

**What I checked.**
- **Pre-registration:** the commit is `e3b87ca24c80df1e6c0f7e1f4e4fa8bd8fb4b404`, and the file is unmodified (the hash of the HEAD version equals the working file).

- **Dry run of section D:** I built a synthetic campaign with 70-second blocks in the scratchpad, never under `data/`. `validate_campaign` exited 1 with six "block too short" failures, and `run_campaign` refused with exit 1. That is the intended behaviour.

**What I filled in the copy.**

# Claude Code prompt: run the 2026-11 recording campaign, fill the checklist, full test

Paste everything below the line into a **fresh** Claude Code session in the Phantom Scan repo root. Thaer (operator) will be present for the whole run.

---

## 0. Role, purpose, and what you cannot do

You are the run controller and test engineer for the frozen 2026-11 recording campaign of Phantom Scan (lead: Thaer Bayyari). You orchestrate, monitor, log, validate and report. You are **not** in the room and cannot see it.

You cannot, and must never pretend to: be the person who walks or stands still, verify that the room is empty, take photos, read the nodes' serial ports, or touch the nodes. Everything that needs a human is an **ACTION FOR OPERATOR**: print it, wait for Thaer to confirm in chat, and record the confirmation with a system-clock UTC timestamp. **A checklist box is ticked only after the operator confirms it or after you measured it. Never tick, fill or invent a physical fact (emptiness, photos, node positions, who entered, firmware build) on your own.** If a value is unknown, write `[NOT PROVIDED]`.

Read first, before anything else: `reports/fix-2026-11/HANDOFF-fresh-session.md`, `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md`, `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/RECORDING-DATA-SPEC.md`, `reports/fix-2026-11/09-WAVE3-addendum.md`. Re-verify every claim in them against the current files before relying on it.

## 1. Rules (non-negotiable)

1. **Hardware truth.** Four ESP32-S3 nodes, one Rx antenna each. No AoA, no pose, no body shape; only 1-D on-axis position is meaningful. All outcomes `[TO BE MEASURED]` until measured.
2. **F1 and F2.** The dashboard is a passive observer and never writes to nodes, serial or firmware. You never open a serial port, never flash, never send anything to a node. AI/ML never writes the authoritative verdict.
3. **No code or config changes during the campaign.** From the first recording to the validator result: no edits to production code, no commits, no dashboard restart unless the recording path requires it (see 3.2) and Thaer approves. Record `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --short` (counts only) in `campaign.json` notes at the start and again at the end. If HEAD changed, say so in the report. A new helper script is allowed only if the existing recorder cannot do the job; it lives under `scripts/presence_eval/`, is read-only with respect to nodes and `data/sessions/`, and gets tests.
4. **No threshold changes.** Presence threshold 0.6, tier thresholds 4/7/14, the 0.05 fallback and everything in PS-EVAL-010/-013 stay as they are. Nothing from this run changes them.
5. **Sessions guard.** Before every phase run `python .tmp/tools/sessions_guard.py check .tmp/tools/sessions-baseline-2026-11-05.json`. New files from the live dashboard are reported, never a stop; a modified or deleted pre-existing file is a stop. Never write into `data/sessions/`, never classify or analyse the dashboard's own session files (they have no sidecar and are not evaluation data). Campaign files go only under `data/datasets/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/`.
6. **Ingest authority.** UDP 5005 has exactly one owner. Do not bind it a second time. Tests use port 5099 only (never 5000/5005).
7. **Honesty.** Unmeasured numbers carry `[TO BE MEASURED]`, unverified claims `[UNVERIFIED]`. A negative or refused result (validator refusal, "no candidate met the criteria") is a valid outcome: report it and stop, do not work around it. Do not tune anything on the evaluation blocks. Do not claim accuracy, detection rate, false-alarm rate, "confidence" or "probability human".
8. **Both failure directions** are reported separately: under-detection (person present, no marker) and ghost target (empty room, marker or EMPTY verdict).
9. **Be quiet.** Long waits are done with a background process and sparse polling, one short status line per poll. Do not narrate each poll.
10. Do not push. Do not delete anything. Commit messages (if any are asked for later) end with the attribution lines required by the session.

## 2. Phase 0, pre-flight (about 15 min)

Create a task list. Then, one step at a time, stop on the first red:

1. `git rev-parse HEAD`, `git log -1 -- reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md`: print the prereg commit hash (reported earlier as `e3b87ca`; verify, do not trust). Confirm the file is tracked and unmodified (`git status`).
2. Run the full suites with `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1 -Label campaign-pre` and show for each: TS (expect 450/450), `tsc` (0 errors), Python (expect 545 passed + 5 skipped), F1/F2 (3/3), AI-firewall whole file (46/46) and filtered (11/11), sessions guard. Paste the **last 25 raw lines of each** into chat. If any count differs from the expectation, stop and report.
3. Find out how recording actually works today. Read `scripts/record_dataset.py --help` and the dashboard's "Training Data Capture" code path. Report, with file:line: does the recorder support an auto-stop timer; does it need UDP 5005 itself (conflict with the live dashboard) or does it go through the dashboard; what files it writes (`*.jsonl`, `*.summary.json`, parts); what label values it accepts. Pick **one** recording path and tell Thaer why. If the path requires stopping or restarting the dashboard, ask Thaer first and wait for a yes.
4. ACTION FOR OPERATOR (print as a numbered list, wait for confirmation of each, then record): firmware build of both nodes identical (Thaer reads the boot logs and tells you the build id; you do not read serial), node positions and N1-N2 distance in metres (the repo's `config/room.json` says 4 m, older docs say 3 m: ask which is true today), router position, fan position and marks A/B and the walk path taped, cables taped, **photo BEFORE taken** (ask for the file names), warm start (nodes and router on at least 60 min), phone in airplane mode.
5. Live smoke test, about 10 minutes, **not campaign data**: confirm both known node IDs (29052, 44260) plus the other two nodes appear, each node about 64-67 fps, RSSI steady. Then a short "scan" recording into `data/datasets/smoke-2026-11/<stamp>/` (NOT under the campaign folder): 2 min empty (operator outside), 2 min walking, 2 min still. Report per node fps and RSSI median per minute, and what the live display showed in each phase (movement shown, EMPTY shown, no data shown). This verifies the pipeline end to end, nothing more; state explicitly that it says nothing about accuracy and is not evaluation data. If anything is odd (node missing, fps collapse, a still person shown as EMPTY), report it and ask Thaer whether to continue.
6. Create `data/datasets/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/` (suggest `c1-<YYYYMMDD>`), `blocks/`, and `campaign.json` per section 4 of the data spec, filled only with confirmed facts: `prereg_commit` (verified hash), `nodes_expected` `[1,2,3,4]` unless Thaer says otherwise, `node_placement`, `firmware.git_hash` (the operator-confirmed build id or `[NOT PROVIDED]`), `wifi_channel`, `ap_bssid` (from the dashboard if it reports them, else ask), `operator`, `notes`.

## 3. Phase 1, the campaign (about 3 h 15 min, fixed order, ONE link state)

Blocks, from the data spec (minimum / plan with margin):

| # | block_type / folder | label | min / plan | condition |
|--|--|--|--|--|
| 1 | `empty_1` / `01_empty_1` | empty | 60 / 62 min | nobody in the room, fan off, **entry log required** |
| 2 | `fan_alone` / `02_fan_alone` | empty_fan | 5 / 6 | fan on at its mark, nobody in room, entry log required |
| 3 | `walking` / `03_walking` | walking | 5 / 6 | 1 person, fan off, slow steady walk along the taped path |
| 4 | `still` / `04_still` | still | 5 / 6 | 1 person, fan off, stand still on mark A facing the baseline |
| 5 | `empty_2` / `05_empty_2` | empty | 60 / 62 | as block 1, **entry log required** |
| 6 | `fan_with_person` / `06_fan_with_person` | still or walking (Thaer chooses BEFORE starting; write it in block.json) | 10 / 11 | 1 person, fan on |

For **every** block:

1. Print the ACTION FOR OPERATOR (leave the room / take position / fan on or off) and wait for confirmation. Record the confirmation time (UTC, system clock).
2. Run the sessions guard. Read the current RSSI per node and fps; if any node already differs by 6 dB or more from the campaign-start values, do not start: report it.
3. Start the recording with the chosen recording path, with an auto-stop at the planned duration. Run it in the background. One recording per block.
4. Monitor while it runs, read-only: tail the recorder's output file (do not bind UDP) and compute per node, every 15 s, the RSSI median and the frame rate. Reuse the constants and functions in `scripts/presence_eval/epochs.py` (`LINK_STEP_DB = 6`, rate collapse 0.7 x the node's reference rate, held-ness rules); do not reimplement them. Print one short line per 5 minutes unless something changes.
5. **LINK CHANGE.** If a held RSSI step of 6 dB or more or a held rate collapse appears on any node: stop the recording immediately, write the event (UTC, node, rssi before/after, fps before/after) to `link_change_events` and the log, tell Thaer, and wait 2 minutes. That block is invalid. Ask Thaer whether anything was touched; then start a **new block** (new recorder file) with a new `link_state_id` unless the level has demonstrably returned. If this happens in block 1 or 5, say plainly that the hour must be repeated. Never continue the same label across a change and never merge parts across it.
6. **Entry log** (`entry-log.txt`, lines `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ <who> <what>`): first line `room checked empty` only after the operator confirmed it, last line at the end of the block. Any door, corridor or entry event is written only when Thaer tells you, with the time he gives (or your clock time, marked as such). You never write an event that nobody reported.
7. When the block ends, write `block.json` per the data spec, from facts: recording base name, start/stop UTC taken from the recording itself (first and last frame time) and the operator's confirmed times, `link_state_id`, person_count, person_present, fan_on, fan_position, subject, `link_state_notes` (the RSSI you measured, plus anything Thaer reported), `link_change_events`, `entry_log`, notes. Verify the recorder's `*.summary.json` exists for every part.
8. Block summary line to chat: planned vs recorded duration (from frames), per node frames/s and RSSI start/end/median, events, files written. Then the next block.

Do not trim recordings by memory; if trimming is needed, only by the log, and say so.

## 4. Phase 2, validate, analyse, report

1. Copy the whole campaign folder to a second location outside the repo's `data/` (ask Thaer where; if he does not say, `D:\WIFI csi\phanotm\campaign-backup-<campaign_id>` after confirming the path is acceptable). Verify the copy with sha256 for every file.
2. ACTION FOR OPERATOR: photo AFTER taken (file names), any node moved? (yes/no and what), end RSSI per node can be read by you from the data.
3. `python -m scripts.presence_eval.validate_campaign <campaign folder>`. If it refuses, paste the exact refusal, say which block and which gate, **stop**, and do not run the analysis. A refusal is a valid outcome.
4. Only if the validator passes: `python -m scripts.presence_eval.run_campaign <campaign folder>`. Paste its report as written (every number is labelled measured or `[TO BE MEASURED]`). Report the C4 empty-versus-empty gate first; if C4 fails, nothing else is a result. Then C1, C1b, C2, C2b, C3, C5, C6, with C7 report-only. State plainly which candidates met or failed which criteria, using the frozen wording. If no candidate met the criteria, write "no candidate met the criteria" and stop; make no accuracy claim.
5. Both failure directions in seconds per block, as C7 requires (report-only): under-detection and ghost.
6. Full suites again with `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1 -Label campaign-post`: same six results as phase 0. Compare counts; explain any difference. Sessions guard again. `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --short` counts again; state whether anything changed since the start.

## 5. The filled checklist (a required output)

Create `reports/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/CHECKLIST-FILLED.md` mirroring every field and box of the printed sheet `Phantom-Scan-Campaign-Checklist` (sections A to D and the six-block table): header (date, operator, campaign_id, link_state_id, subject), each section-A item, the six blocks with start/stop UTC and the plan, the RSSI/fps lines, the LINK CHANGE events, the section-C file checks, and the section-D items.

For every box write one of: `[x] measured` (with the value and source file), `[x] operator-confirmed <UTC time>`, `[ ] not done`, or `[NOT PROVIDED]`. Never `[x]` without one of the first two. Add a table "values taken from data vs values from the operator". Also list every deviation from the plan with time and reason. This file is committed only if Thaer asks.

## 6. Final report (required format)

Write `reports/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/RUN-REPORT.md` and give a compact summary in chat:

1. A table, one row per block: block_id, planned vs recorded minutes (from frames), link_state_id, link-change events, files, validator verdict, blocking or non-blocking.
2. Smoke-test results (clearly marked not evaluation data).
3. Validator result verbatim; run_campaign result verbatim (or "not run, because the validator refused", with the refusal).
4. Suites before and after: the last 25 raw lines of each in chat; full output stored under `.tmp/suite-output/` with sha256.
5. Sessions guard before/after; HEAD and working-tree counts before/after.
6. What you could not verify, each tagged `[UNVERIFIED]` (for example the room's emptiness, the firmware build, the operator's confirmations).
7. Failure directions: under-detection and ghost, in seconds per block, each labelled measured or `[TO BE MEASURED]`.
8. Open questions for Thaer, and the recommended next step. Do not recommend a threshold value.

Do not push. Do not flash. Do not open serial. Do not change any threshold. Do not delete any file.

## Step 3: how recording works today

| | Dashboard "Training Data Capture" | `scripts/record_dataset.py` |
|--|--|--|
| Auto-stop timer | **None.** There is only a 10 s countdown at start (`dataset-recorder-panel.tsx:17`, `:49-62`). Stopping is manual. | **Yes**, `--seconds` (`record_dataset.py:322`). |
| UDP 5005 | Not bound by the recorder. It records samples the running dashboard already ingests (`websocket.ts:2673-2675`). Start and stop come from the panel as authenticated websocket messages (`websocket.ts:465-471`, auth at `:357-359`). | **Binds 5005 itself** (`record_dataset.py:263-267`), so the dashboard would have to be stopped for the whole 3 h. |
| Files | `data/datasets/<roomId>/<roomId>__<subject>__<label>__<UTC>.jsonl`. A new part starts every 100,000 frames (`dataset-recorder.ts:55`), about every 6.4 min at 4 × 65 fps, so a 62-min block gives about 10 parts. Each part gets a `.summary.json` (`dataset-recorder.ts:209`, `:442-450`). | `<data-root>/<room>/…jsonl` (`:247`), one file plus `.summary.json` (`:307`). |
| Labels | `DATASET_LABELS` (`dataset-recorder.ts:32-40`) includes `empty`, `empty_fan`, `still`, `walking`. | Same list (`record_dataset.py:45`). |
| Demo mode | Records nothing while a demo is active (`websocket.ts:2673`). | n/a |

**My recommendation is the dashboard path.** It needs no dashboard stop or restart (rule 3), it keeps a single owner of UDP 5005, and it matches what the checklist describes: a 10 s countdown, then stopping from outside the room. There is no auto-stop, so I'll watch the frame times read-only and tell you the moment each block reaches its planned length, and you press Stop.

## Claude — 2026-11-05 08:43:59Z

All three code checks are done. Nothing here needs a stop, but the trim question needs a decision from you before block 1.

**1. Room ID is only a folder name and a label.** It is used in exactly four places, all in the recorder:
- the folder name (`server/lib/dataset-recorder.ts:410`)
- the file base name (`:327`)
- the part header (`:424`)
- the status broadcast and log line (`:391`, `websocket.ts:2625`)

Starting a recording only checks the request and opens a file (`websocket.ts:2618-2632`, `dataset-recorder.ts:320-341`). It does not reset or set baselines, calibration, the detector, thresholds or tiers. The other `roomId` in `shared/schema.ts:269` is a field in the AI sidecar's advisory message, which is a separate thing and unrelated to this panel.

**2. Part boundaries can't drop a frame by design.** When a part is full, the recorder flushes the old part, opens the next one, and then writes the incoming frame into the new part, all in the same call (`dataset-recorder.ts:349-356`). I'll still measure the gap on the first part boundary of block 1, per node, against the normal frame interval, and report the number.

**3. `start_utc`/`stop_utc`: neither script trims.**
- **`validate_campaign`:** it takes the duration and the minimum from the first and last frame times of the recording (`campaign.py:141-146`). From `block.json` it only checks that start is before stop (`:118-120`). It warns, without failing, if the stated span differs from the recorded span by more than 120 s (`:147`). It also checks that entry-log lines fall within the block's start/stop ± 300 s (`:101`). So an extra walk-out of 5–15 s is accepted.
- **`run_campaign`:** it loads every frame of every part (`run_campaign.py:210-212`, via `load_tables(parts, None, None)`). Those two `None` arguments are cache paths, not a time window (`tables.py:44`). **It does not trim by `stop_utc`, so the walk-out seconds go into the analysis.**

**How big the walk-out effect is.** About 5–15 s means 3–10 windows of 1.5 s. A 6-min block has about 240 windows, so that is up to 4 %; the 11-min block has about 440, so up to 2 %. In the still block, those walk-out windows look like motion, which would make "still" look slightly better than it is. In the walking and fan-with-person blocks the person is moving anyway.

**Proposal for the trim, with no code changes during the campaign.**
1. During the campaign: you call out "walking out" as you leave, and I log the time to the second in that block's `entry-log.txt` (also written for blocks 3, 4 and 6) and in the `block.json` notes. `stop_utc` is the time you press Stop.
2. After the campaign: if you approve, I enter the walk-out as a deviation in the pre-registration's section 6 table and commit it.
3. Then I run `run_campaign` exactly as frozen, which is the pre-registered primary result.
4. Optionally, I add a small tested analysis-window option that ends each block at its logged walk-out time. Its output would be labelled **exploratory**: it can't meet or fail a criterion, and it only shows whether those seconds change anything.

Do you approve this?

**Notes for later:**
- **Calibrate Empty Room:** neither of us will press it. I'll also check that the dashboard log doesn't show it during the campaign.
- **Before block 1** I'll remind you to disable sleep and hibernate and to pause Windows updates. I can read the current power settings with `powercfg /query`, which changes nothing, if you want that check too.

One correction to the item 1 advice, so a node isn't rebooted by accident:
- **Opening a serial monitor normally resets the board.** `idf.py -p COMx monitor` toggles the reset lines on connect, which restarts the node. That changes the link (a block would be invalid), restarts the 60-minute warm start, and may move the node to a new link state.
- **If you read the build id today:** do it **before** the warm-start clock starts, or use the no-reset option. My notes say `idf.py … monitor --no-reset`, but I haven't verified that this flag exists in your ESP-IDF version, so check `idf.py monitor --help` first.
- **If in doubt:** answer "item 1: not provided". The run can go ahead, and the report will mark the firmware build `[UNVERIFIED]`.

## Claude — 2026-11-05 11:03:21Z

Retracted. Answers below, all read-only; nothing changed.

**1. What "scan running" means.** It is the dashboard's Start Scan, which sends the websocket message `startProcessing` (`server/lib/websocket.ts:428-431`) and runs `startProcessing()` (`:730-819`). Starting it:
- sets `isProcessing = true` (`:741`);
- **resets the live detector state**: heatmap, background subtractors, motion gate, Doppler, tracking, sample buffer and target artifacts (`:742-751`). This is a fresh warm-up of the live detector. Baselines and settings on disk are not changed, and no calibration is pressed;
- starts `processFrame` every 50 ms, about 20 Hz (`:807-809`). That is the only place detection happens, and it returns immediately while no scan runs (`:1055`);
- **auto-starts the dashboard's own session recording** (`:812-814`). Its buffer is capped at 5,000 rows (`:1603`). When the scan is **stopped**, a session report is saved into `data/sessions/` (`:845-847`). That is the dashboard's own output: the guard lists it as new, and it is never analysed.

**2. What gets recorded.** The campaign recorder writes **only** `t, n, rssi, a` per frame (`server/lib/dataset-recorder.ts:353-355`), with no tier, excess, trends or markers. It records in `acceptSample()` **before, and independent of**, any scan state (`websocket.ts:2671-2686`). The frame timestamp comes from ingest, not from processing. **The raw amplitude vectors are recorded identically with and without the scan.** Tier, excess, trends and markers exist only in the scan's session rows, which are not campaign data.

**3. How `run_campaign` computes C7.** Offline, from candidate scores calculated on the recorded frames:
- each threshold is the 1−α quantile of the **first empty block's** scores (`scripts/presence_eval/run_campaign.py:156`);
- under-detection = walking or still windows at or below the threshold × 1.5 s, and ghost = empty_2 or fan-alone windows above it (`:134-141`, `:161-163`).

C7 does not use the live gate or any recorded derived field, so **running the scan cannot change it.**

⚠ **Wording mismatch to log:** the pre-registration describes C7 as seconds "with no **marker**" or "with a marker" (`PREREG…md:57`). Live markers are not in the campaign files; they exist only in the dashboard's session output, which we never analyse. So the reported C7 is the offline candidate threshold version, and **a marker-based C7 is not available** (`[TO BE MEASURED]`). I'll state this in the report as a wording deviation, unless you want it handled differently.

**4. Does the scan change anything the offline evaluation uses?** **No.** The evaluation reads only the recorded `t, n, rssi, a`, and those are captured before processing. The only indirect risk is CPU load from the 20 Hz processing loop affecting ingest timing, meaning fewer frames or longer gaps. That is measurable.

## Recommendation: scan ON, if the re-smoke shows no ingest cost

| | scan ON | scan OFF |
|--|--|--|
| Offline evaluation (C1–C7) | identical | identical |
| Live-display evidence for both failure directions | yes: your qualitative notes in person blocks | none (blank display) |
| Ingest risk | 20 Hz processing load. Check: median fps per node within ±2 of the earlier smoke (65–67), longest gap < 0.5 s (earlier smoke: 0.06–0.26 s), no zero-frame seconds | none (baseline measured: 65–67 fps, gaps ≤ 0.26 s) |
| Side effects | live detector reset once at start; one session file in `data/sessions/` when the scan finally stops (after the campaign) | none |

If the re-smoke fails that check, I'll recommend OFF.

## Claude — 2026-11-08 17:33:28Z

N3's collapses are specific to N3 and look like a uniformly lower frame rate, with no RSSI fall behind them. Host-side recorder or UDP problems are ruled out. I can't separate a node-side cause from local interference with this data. Nothing was written, trimmed or analysed for detection, and there was no serial, dashboard or code or config change.

**Timing correction:** the first event began at 16:05:01Z, not about 16:05:20Z as I said earlier. The monitor's "3104 s" was when it noticed the event.

## Measured

**1 and 2. Events and part boundaries.** Part length is 388 s.

| Onset UTC | fps | Duration | Nearest part boundary |
|--|--|--|--|
| 16:05:01 | 56→38 | 25 s | 116 s |
| 16:11:58 | 64→40 | 14 s | 84 s |
| 16:30:20 | 63→42 | 17 s | 112 s |
| 16:40:30 | 63→37 | 10 s | 112 s |
| 16:47:00 | 58→40 | 26 s | 111 s |

- **Boundaries:** the mean distance to the nearest boundary is 107 s. Randomly placed onsets would average 99 s. None of the five is within 20 s of a boundary, and N3 averages 61.3 fps in the 10 s after boundaries against 60.2 overall. The recorder's part rotation is ruled out.
- **Duration:** this is my definition, the time until the 5 s median fps is back to at least 0.85 of baseline for 10 s. The detector needs a 10 s window, so 10 s is the floor. The events total about 92 s of 7,701 s.

**3. Cross-node.** In the first two events N1, N2 and N4 are flat (1.00 to 1.01×).
- **Later events:** in events 3 to 5 N4 and N2 dip slightly, down to 0.85× for N4 at 16:40:30.
- **Correlation:** after removing a 121 s moving mean, N3 correlates with the others at only +0.56 to +0.63. The other three correlate with each other at +0.90 to +0.96. So there is one shared slow component and a separate N3 component.
- **Shared swing:** the other nodes swing together between about 57 and 68 fps, with troughs near 15:40Z and 16:53Z. That swing is not an N3 event.

**4. N3 RSSI.** It is flat before, during and after each event. Medians are -54, -52, -54 and -52, -52, -51.5, with the last two at -51 throughout. RSSI does not fall with fps. The whole-recording correlation is -0.26, and -0.08 after high-pass.

**5. Inter-frame intervals on N3.**
- **Before an event:** median 15–17 ms.
- **During an event:** median 20–28 ms, 99th percentile 39–41 ms, and zero gaps over 60 ms in four of five events. The other has one gap of 65 ms.
- **Whole recording:** 99% of the time is outside gaps over 60 ms.

So during a collapse the rate is uniformly lower, not a few long dropouts. The recording has one zero-frame second and two seconds at or below 30 fps.

**6. Time of day.** All five events fall between 16:03Z and 16:50Z, a window of about 45 min. None occurs in the first 50 min or the last 30 min.
- **Low periods:** N3 also sat low relative to the others from 17:00Z to 17:08Z, where the ratio reached 0.85, without a detector event.
- **No RSSI steps:** the detector found zero RSSI-step events on N3.

**7. Earlier attempt `…T151149Z`.** It has N3 data, but it is only 78 s long (15:11:49Z to 15:13:07Z), and the rest of the diagnosis couldn't run on it. The detector found zero N3 events in it. Mean fps was N1 64.9, N2 64.7, N3 62.2 and N4 63.9, so nothing stands out. It is too short to say anything about collapses.

## Which hypothesis the data supports

| Hypothesis | Verdict |
|--|--|
| Host-side (recorder or UDP drops) | Measured: not supported. Nodes 1, 2 and 4 are unchanged in events 1 and 2, and no event sits at a boundary. Small partial dips on N4 and N2 in events 3 to 5 leave a minor common component. |
| Weak link, RSSI fade | Measured: not supported. N3 is the weakest node (-51 to -54, against -26 to -46 for the others). It stays flat and does not fall with fps. |
| Power or reboot | Measured: no reboot, since there are no multi-second silences. Marginal-power rate throttling is `[UNVERIFIED]`, as there is no supply measurement. |
| Interference or airtime near N3 | `[UNVERIFIED]`, not excluded. The 45 min clustering and the N3-only pattern fit a transient source near N3. No spectrum data exists. |
| Node-side (N3's own radio or firmware pacing) | `[UNVERIFIED]`, not excluded. It matches the N3-specific, uniform rate drop. I can't tell lower sending from alternate frames lost without a frame counter, and the records carry none. |

Best fit: a rate effect local to N3 (its radio, its sending, or its immediate surroundings) on top of a slow swing that all nodes share. Which of those it is can't be told from this recording. I'm not recommending any fix.

# Claude Code prompt: Gate 2 decisions → Wave 3 + recording-readiness

Paste everything below the line into Claude Code, in the Phantom Scan repo root.

---

## 0. Role and ground rules

You are working on Phantom Scan (Thaer Bayyari, lead; supervisor Dr. Ahmad Al-Nawasrah; stakeholder JODDB). Wave 0–2 are done and the owner has now made his Gate 2 decisions (section 2). This prompt covers Wave 3 plus the preparation needed so that the new recordings can be analysed the day they exist.

Non-negotiable rules (unchanged from earlier waves):

1. **Hardware truth.** Two-node fused is the primary mode. One Rx antenna per node. No AoA, no pose, no body shape. Only 1-D on-axis energy-balance position. Anything else must carry the existing caveat pattern (`getLivePositionCaveat`, `canPresentLiveTargetPosition`, `canRenderConfidentHuman`).
2. **F1 and F2 stay intact.** AI/ML never writes the authoritative verdict. The dashboard never writes upstream to nodes, serial or firmware. Re-run `tests/test_invariants.py::test_f1_*`, `test_f2_*`, and the AI-firewall tests explicitly after every item that touches detection, gating, AI integration, display, ingest or protocol.
3. **Honesty.** Unmeasured numbers carry `[TO BE MEASURED]`. Claims you did not verify in this session carry `[UNVERIFIED]`. Re-verify every claim from earlier reports against the current files before acting on it. Negative results ("could not reproduce", "no effect") are valid outcomes; do not invent a fix.
4. **No safety constant changes.** Do not change the presence threshold (0.6), room tier thresholds (4/7/14), or the fallback 0.05. PS-EVAL-010 and PS-EVAL-013 stay with the owner. Flag, do not guess.
5. **Do not touch the running dashboard or the user's live data.** All server tests use the isolated port 5099 guard. Never bind 5000/5005 in tests. Never write into `data/sessions/` except through the verified restore procedure. Before and after any test run, confirm `data/sessions/_index.json` sha256 prefix is still `0261130fef4cfed9` and the file count is unchanged (730 plus the 5 quarantined in `.tmp/quarantine-sessions-2026-11-03/`). If it differs, stop and report.
6. **No flashing, no serial writes.** Firmware items are host-tested only. Nothing in this wave may open a serial port for writing.
7. **One item at a time.** Fix, test, full suite green, report that item, then the next. Stop on the first red.
8. **Both failure directions.** Every item that can change what is shown must be checked against real recordings for under-detection (person present, no marker) and for ghost targets (empty room, marker shown). State which direction each change can affect and show the numbers.
9. **Full suites before and after each item, pasted in full, not paraphrased:** the TypeScript suite, `tsc`, and the Python suite. Baseline from Wave 2: TS 444/444, tsc 0 errors, Python 342 passed + 5 opt-in skips, F1/F2 3/3. Any change in these counts must be explained in the item report.
10. **Quantify tradeoffs** (dB, latency in ms, cycles or bytes of flash/RAM for firmware, % of frames affected). No qualitative-only statements.

Work in `reports/fix-2026-11/`. Update `80-issues-ledger`, `89-merge-gate` and `90-test-report` after each item. Add a new `09-WAVE3-addendum.md` for this wave.

## 3. Work items (in this order)

### Item A — PS-FIX-38: stop documenting an unsafe threshold rule

Docs still describe the presence threshold as "mean + k·sigma" derived from a short run. The committed derivation used a 60 s empty recording (mean 0.49, σ 0.027, max 0.58 → 0.6). A 60 s window cannot support a k·σ rule because the real empty-vs-empty drift is larger than within-recording σ.

- `grep` the docs, README, dashboard help text, exported-report templates and code comments for the rule. List every hit with file and line.
- Rewrite each hit to say what was actually done (single short empty recording, value 0.6, provisional) and what real recalibration requires (live empty-room soak ≥ 60 min with entry log, plus a negative control with fan/vibration, using the project's calibration tooling). Mark the false-alarm and detection figures `[TO BE MEASURED]`.
- Add a doc-lint test that fails if the phrase "mean + k" (any spacing/σ/sigma variant) reappears in docs or exported-report templates without the `[TO BE MEASURED]` or "provisional" qualifier in the same paragraph.
- Do not change the threshold value.

### Item B — PS-FIX-37: make the frame-alternation finding reproducible

Background (from the owner's reviewer, exploratory, scratch scripts outside the repo, so `[UNVERIFIED]` until you reproduce it): on all four nodes in session `s6`, correlation between a frame and the frame two later is higher than with its immediate neighbour (N3 lag-2 0.82 vs lag-1 0.72), and RSSI differences are smaller at lag 2 than lag 1. This fits about 2 frames per 30 ms ping. Recomputing features on same-parity substreams did **not** remove the empty-vs-empty drift (AUC stays ≈ 0.93–1.00 for perturb/decor), so interleaving is not the main cause of the between-session drift. It does lower the decor level by about 25–37 % (N3 0.282 → 0.178).

- Add `scripts/presence_eval/frame_parity.py` (read-only, reads `data/sessions/`): per node and session, lag-1/lag-2/lag-3 correlation of demeaned subcarrier vectors, lag-1/lag-2 RSSI difference, inter-frame interval histogram, and the same-parity decor vs all-frames decor.
- Run it on every available multi-node session and write the table to `reports/fix-2026-11/results/frame-parity.md`. If you cannot reproduce the alternation, say so plainly and show what you tried.
- State explicitly in the report: this does not explain the drift; it affects decor level and the choice of lag; it motivates Item G (transmitter field).
- Serial counters: do **not** write code that opens a serial port. Write `docs/serial-counter-capture.md`, a short procedure the owner follows by hand (which firmware counters to read, how to paste the log), plus a parser `scripts/presence_eval/parse_serial_counters.py` that reads a pasted text file. This keeps F2 intact.

### Item C — PS-FIX-40: resampling aliases the ping cadence

Finding from Wave 2: the 50 Hz resample aliases the 33.3 Hz ping cadence to 16.7 Hz. Frames arrive at about 65 fps per node, roughly 2 per ping.

- Locate every resample or fixed-rate assumption in `phantom/dsp.py` and the TS pipeline (grep for 50, `fs`, `resample`, `decimate`, `interp`). List them with file:line.
- Demonstrate the alias with a synthetic test (33.3 Hz impulse train through the current resampler) and with one real session (spectrum before/after). Include the respiration (0.1–0.5 Hz) and cardiac (0.8–2.0 Hz) bands: show whether the alias lands inside either.
- Propose the fix: resample to a rate ≥ 64 Hz (justify the exact choice with the Nyquist margin for 33.3 Hz and its harmonics) or avoid fixed resampling and use timestamp-aware processing. Implement the smallest change that removes the alias. Keep the old path behind a flag only if a test needs it.
- Regression tests: synthetic alias test; band-energy test; existing DSP tests unchanged.
- Check both failure directions: replay the same real empty and walking sessions before/after and report presence-gate output counts. If the numbers move, report by how much and do not touch the threshold.

### Item D — small hardening

Wrap the call to `phyGuard.observe` in `server/lib/udp-server.ts` in try/catch so a guard fault can never drop or delay a frame. Log once per 1000 errors, not per frame. Add a test that injects a throwing guard and proves the frame is still ingested. Run the full TS suite.

### Item E — PS-FIX-16: fail-closed outputs

- Define, in a short design section first, which outputs fail closed (no data, stale data, node missing, rate collapse, PHY mismatch in shadow mode) and what the user sees instead (state chip NO DATA / SETTLING, never EMPTY).
- Implement with the existing caveat pattern. Never present "empty" as a verdict from missing data.
- Replay check on real sessions, both directions: (a) the fail-closed rules must not suppress a real walking segment; (b) they must not produce a marker on an empty or no-data segment. Show counts per session.
- F1/F2/AI-firewall tests re-run explicitly.

### Item F — PS-FIX-14: firmware robustness (host-tested only)

- Bounded calibration retry (today it can retry without bound) and a CSI stall watchdog, in `edge_processing.c` / `csi_collector.c` as appropriate. Verify the current behaviour in the source first; do not rely on this prompt's description.
- Everything new behind a Kconfig option, **default OFF**. No behaviour change in a default build. Prove it: build the host tests with the option off and show the compiled logic is unchanged (diff of symbol list or test outputs).
- Host tests for the pure logic (`edge_dsp_pure.c` style). Report added flash and RAM in bytes and worst-case cycles per frame for the watchdog check, as estimates marked `[UNVERIFIED]` unless measured on target.
- Do not flash. State that it has never run on hardware.

### Item G — PS-FIX-36: header fields for bandwidth, sig_mode, transmitter

Goal: let the host tell the two frame types per ping apart and enforce PHY assumptions later (PS-FIX-13, still not approved).

- Design first: new fields, byte layout in the 32-byte header (or the smallest compatible extension), version negotiation, how old firmware/new server and new firmware/old server behave. Quantify bytes added per frame and per second at 65 fps × 4 nodes.
- Dispatch order: check the longer / more specific magic first. Add the regression test for exactly that bug class, plus tests for old-format frames still parsing identically (byte-for-byte golden frames from real recordings).
- Implement the server parser and firmware pack function with host tests only. **Not flashed.** Shadow-mode PHY guard may read the new fields if present; it must still never drop frames.
- Document the single-owner ingest rule is unchanged (UDP 5005, no shared bind).

### Item H — PS-FIX-12: rate-scaled timing (design note only, then STOP)

Write `reports/fix-2026-11/10-PS-FIX-12-design.md` only. Cover: every time constant that is currently counted in frames and silently assumes a fixed rate (list with file:line), how each would scale with the measured per-node rate, the failure cases when the rate is wrong or changes mid-session (link-state epochs), quantified effect on window lengths in seconds at 11, 41–43 and 65 fps, and the test plan including replay on real sessions in both failure directions. Do not write production code. End with the exact questions the owner must answer at Gate 3.

### Item I — licence hygiene

Re-verify PS-FIX-24 and -26 against current files. Confirm in `06-licences` that `pose_v1` and any MM-Fi-derived weights, data or code are not in the repo's deliverable paths, exported reports, docs or the dashboard bundle. Add a check script that fails if such paths or strings appear in the report templates or the packaged build. Report; do not delete anything without asking.

## 4. Recording-readiness (no new recordings exist yet)

The blocker for detection work is data, not code. Prepare the repo so analysis starts the moment recordings land.

1. **Pre-registration file first.** Create `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md` and commit it **before** any new recording is analysed. It states the success criteria in advance: required AUC lower confidence bound for each candidate feature, the C4 empty-vs-empty gate (empty vs second empty must stay near 0.5; if not, the link state changed and the block is invalid), minimum durations, and what counts as failure. Use numbers derived from the existing results (no candidate passed before); where you cannot justify a number, write `[OWNER TO SET]` instead of picking one.
2. **Recording protocol as data spec.** Describe the folder layout, naming, metadata sidecar fields (block type, start/stop UTC, entry log, link-state notes, fan on/off, person count, node placement) and the LINK CHANGE rule (≥ 6 dB RSSI shift or fps collapse → stop, log, wait 2 min, new block). Blocks: ≥ 60 min empty with entry log; fan alone 5 min; walking 5 min; still 5 min; second empty ≥ 60 min; fan with a person present. All inside one link state.
3. **Ingest validator.** A script that takes a new session folder and refuses (non-zero exit, clear message) when: sidecar missing, block shorter than required (PS-FIX-11 rules), fps outside the expected band, RSSI step ≥ 6 dB inside a block, node missing, or epochs not segmented. It never writes into `data/sessions/`.
4. **One-command analysis.** `scripts/presence_eval/run_campaign.py <folder>` runs epoch segmentation, parity-aware features (Item B), causal self-baseline movement score (repo rule: ≥ 80 % of lookback windows), AUC with block-bootstrap CIs, the C4 gate, and writes a report with every number labelled measured or `[TO BE MEASURED]`. Dry-run it on existing sessions and show it behaves the same as the current repo tooling (same numbers within stated tolerance).
5. Do not change any threshold from the output of this script in this wave.

## 5. Final report (required format)

Write `reports/fix-2026-11/09-WAVE3-addendum.md` and give me, in chat:

1. A table, one row per item A–I plus section 4: ID, status (done / design only / skipped / blocked), commit hash, tests added, **blocking or non-blocking**, failure direction checked (under-detection / ghost / neither) with numbers.

4. The `_index.json` sha256 prefix and file count before and after.
5. A list of what you could not verify and why, each tagged `[UNVERIFIED]`.
6. The Gate 3 questions for the owner (including those from Item H).
7. Any red flag you found in earlier reports' claims (things that drifted).

Do not push. Do not flash. Do not delete files without asking first.

GateGuard facts for the new scratchpad file `sessions_guard.py`:

## Claude — 2026-11-04 15:03:38Z

The runner is built and tested, and I have not run any step in your repository. Nothing is staged and HEAD is still `0e61373`. I'm waiting for you to say the `[OWNER TO SET]` items are final before I run step P.

**Where it is.** `.tmp/commit-steps/run_steps.py` does nothing without `--go`. With `--go` it does the following:
- Checks that `.tmp/pre-wave3-backup` exists and that `MANIFEST.sha256` verifies. It did when I checked a moment ago.
- Refuses to start if anything is already staged.
- Snapshots the working tree, then runs P, O1–O8, M-01…M-08, M-10…M-14 and R in order.
- Before each step it prints the expected stat. After staging it prints `git diff --cached --stat` and commits only if the two are identical.
- On any difference it stops with exit 3, leaves the index staged for you to inspect and commits nothing for that step.
- It never pushes and never touches `data/`, serial ports or the flasher.
- At the end it prints the final proof: every pre-commit file equals its `HEAD` blob, plus `git diff HEAD --stat -- . :!data :!node_modules` and `git status`.

**What I tested.**
- **Dry run on the real repo:** the expected stats parse for all steps.
- **Throwaway clone:** step P staged and committed with identical stats. I then altered O2's expected stat by one line (74 → 75 insertions). The run printed both stats, reported the mismatch, stopped, and left O2 staged but uncommitted.
- **Not tested:** the full end-to-end run with the final proof, because the clone has no uncommitted working tree to compare against. It is untested until the real run.

For P and R, the expected stat is computed on a scratch index at run time, because 88b has no exact figure for them. P currently expects 1 file, +83 lines.

**What I need from you.** Are the `[OWNER TO SET]` / `[OWNER TO CONFIRM]` items in `PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md` final as written? **What I checked.**
- **Pre-registration:** the commit is `e3b87ca24c80df1e6c0f7e1f4e4fa8bd8fb4b404`, and the file is unmodified (the hash of the HEAD version equals the working file).
- **Block minimums:** the sheet's 60 / 5 / 5 / 5 / 60 / 10 min match `campaign.py`, and the planned 62+6+6+6+62+11 adds up to the 153 min on the sheet.
- **Labels:** `empty`, `empty_fan`, `still` and `walking` are accepted by both the dashboard recorder and `record_dataset.py`.
- **Tests:** the campaign, pose and invariant tests give 55 passed.
- **Dry run of section D:** I built a synthetic campaign with 70-second blocks in the scratchpad, never under `data/`. `validate_campaign` exited 1 with six "block too short" failures, and `run_campaign` refused with exit 1. That is the intended behaviour.

**What I filled in the copy.**

# Claude Code prompt: run the 2026-11 recording campaign, fill the checklist, full test

Paste everything below the line into a **fresh** Claude Code session in the Phantom Scan repo root. Thaer (operator) will be present for the whole run.

---

## 0. Role, purpose, and what you cannot do

You are the run controller and test engineer for the frozen 2026-11 recording campaign of Phantom Scan (lead: Thaer Bayyari). You orchestrate, monitor, log, validate and report. You are **not** in the room and cannot see it.

You cannot, and must never pretend to: be the person who walks or stands still, verify that the room is empty, take photos, read the nodes' serial ports, or touch the nodes. Everything that needs a human is an **ACTION FOR OPERATOR**: print it, wait for Thaer to confirm in chat, and record the confirmation with a system-clock UTC timestamp. **A checklist box is ticked only after the operator confirms it or after you measured it. Never tick, fill or invent a physical fact (emptiness, photos, node positions, who entered, firmware build) on your own.** If a value is unknown, write `[NOT PROVIDED]`.

Read first, before anything else: `reports/fix-2026-11/HANDOFF-fresh-session.md`, `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md`, `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/RECORDING-DATA-SPEC.md`, `reports/fix-2026-11/09-WAVE3-addendum.md`. Re-verify every claim in them against the current files before relying on it.

## 1. Rules (non-negotiable)

1. **Hardware truth.** Four ESP32-S3 nodes, one Rx antenna each. No AoA, no pose, no body shape; only 1-D on-axis position is meaningful. All outcomes `[TO BE MEASURED]` until measured.
2. **F1 and F2.** The dashboard is a passive observer and never writes to nodes, serial or firmware. You never open a serial port, never flash, never send anything to a node. AI/ML never writes the authoritative verdict.
3. **No code or config changes during the campaign.** From the first recording to the validator result: no edits to production code, no commits, no dashboard restart unless the recording path requires it (see 3.2) and Thaer approves. Record `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --short` (counts only) in `campaign.json` notes at the start and again at the end. If HEAD changed, say so in the report. A new helper script is allowed only if the existing recorder cannot do the job; it lives under `scripts/presence_eval/`, is read-only with respect to nodes and `data/sessions/`, and gets tests.
4. **No threshold changes.** Presence threshold 0.6, tier thresholds 4/7/14, the 0.05 fallback and everything in PS-EVAL-010/-013 stay as they are. Nothing from this run changes them.
5. **Sessions guard.** Before every phase run `python .tmp/tools/sessions_guard.py check .tmp/tools/sessions-baseline-2026-11-05.json`. New files from the live dashboard are reported, never a stop; a modified or deleted pre-existing file is a stop. Never write into `data/sessions/`, never classify or analyse the dashboard's own session files (they have no sidecar and are not evaluation data). Campaign files go only under `data/datasets/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/`.
6. **Ingest authority.** UDP 5005 has exactly one owner. Do not bind it a second time. Tests use port 5099 only (never 5000/5005).
7. **Honesty.** Unmeasured numbers carry `[TO BE MEASURED]`, unverified claims `[UNVERIFIED]`. A negative or refused result (validator refusal, "no candidate met the criteria") is a valid outcome: report it and stop, do not work around it. Do not tune anything on the evaluation blocks. Do not claim accuracy, detection rate, false-alarm rate, "confidence" or "probability human".
8. **Both failure directions** are reported separately: under-detection (person present, no marker) and ghost target (empty room, marker or EMPTY verdict).
9. **Be quiet.** Long waits are done with a background process and sparse polling, one short status line per poll. Do not narrate each poll.
10. Do not push. Do not delete anything. Commit messages (if any are asked for later) end with the attribution lines required by the session.

## 2. Phase 0, pre-flight (about 15 min)

Create a task list. Then, one step at a time, stop on the first red:

1. `git rev-parse HEAD`, `git log -1 -- reports/presence-eval-2026-11/PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md`: print the prereg commit hash (reported earlier as `e3b87ca`; verify, do not trust). Confirm the file is tracked and unmodified (`git status`).
2. Run the full suites with `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1 -Label campaign-pre` and show for each: TS (expect 450/450), `tsc` (0 errors), Python (expect 545 passed + 5 skipped), F1/F2 (3/3), AI-firewall whole file (46/46) and filtered (11/11), sessions guard. Paste the **last 25 raw lines of each** into chat. If any count differs from the expectation, stop and report.
3. Find out how recording actually works today. Read `scripts/record_dataset.py --help` and the dashboard's "Training Data Capture" code path. Report, with file:line: does the recorder support an auto-stop timer; does it need UDP 5005 itself (conflict with the live dashboard) or does it go through the dashboard; what files it writes (`*.jsonl`, `*.summary.json`, parts); what label values it accepts. Pick **one** recording path and tell Thaer why. If the path requires stopping or restarting the dashboard, ask Thaer first and wait for a yes.
4. ACTION FOR OPERATOR (print as a numbered list, wait for confirmation of each, then record): firmware build of both nodes identical (Thaer reads the boot logs and tells you the build id; you do not read serial), node positions and N1-N2 distance in metres (the repo's `config/room.json` says 4 m, older docs say 3 m: ask which is true today), router position, fan position and marks A/B and the walk path taped, cables taped, **photo BEFORE taken** (ask for the file names), warm start (nodes and router on at least 60 min), phone in airplane mode.
5. Live smoke test, about 10 minutes, **not campaign data**: confirm both known node IDs (29052, 44260) plus the other two nodes appear, each node about 64-67 fps, RSSI steady. Then a short "scan" recording into `data/datasets/smoke-2026-11/<stamp>/` (NOT under the campaign folder): 2 min empty (operator outside), 2 min walking, 2 min still. Report per node fps and RSSI median per minute, and what the live display showed in each phase (movement shown, EMPTY shown, no data shown). This verifies the pipeline end to end, nothing more; state explicitly that it says nothing about accuracy and is not evaluation data. If anything is odd (node missing, fps collapse, a still person shown as EMPTY), report it and ask Thaer whether to continue.
6. Create `data/datasets/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/` (suggest `c1-<YYYYMMDD>`), `blocks/`, and `campaign.json` per section 4 of the data spec, filled only with confirmed facts: `prereg_commit` (verified hash), `nodes_expected` `[1,2,3,4]` unless Thaer says otherwise, `node_placement`, `firmware.git_hash` (the operator-confirmed build id or `[NOT PROVIDED]`), `wifi_channel`, `ap_bssid` (from the dashboard if it reports them, else ask), `operator`, `notes`.

## 3. Phase 1, the campaign (about 3 h 15 min, fixed order, ONE link state)

Blocks, from the data spec (minimum / plan with margin):

| # | block_type / folder | label | min / plan | condition |
|--|--|--|--|--|
| 1 | `empty_1` / `01_empty_1` | empty | 60 / 62 min | nobody in the room, fan off, **entry log required** |
| 2 | `fan_alone` / `02_fan_alone` | empty_fan | 5 / 6 | fan on at its mark, nobody in room, entry log required |
| 3 | `walking` / `03_walking` | walking | 5 / 6 | 1 person, fan off, slow steady walk along the taped path |
| 4 | `still` / `04_still` | still | 5 / 6 | 1 person, fan off, stand still on mark A facing the baseline |
| 5 | `empty_2` / `05_empty_2` | empty | 60 / 62 | as block 1, **entry log required** |
| 6 | `fan_with_person` / `06_fan_with_person` | still or walking (Thaer chooses BEFORE starting; write it in block.json) | 10 / 11 | 1 person, fan on |

For **every** block:

1. Print the ACTION FOR OPERATOR (leave the room / take position / fan on or off) and wait for confirmation. Record the confirmation time (UTC, system clock).
2. Run the sessions guard. Read the current RSSI per node and fps; if any node already differs by 6 dB or more from the campaign-start values, do not start: report it.
3. Start the recording with the chosen recording path, with an auto-stop at the planned duration. Run it in the background. One recording per block.
4. Monitor while it runs, read-only: tail the recorder's output file (do not bind UDP) and compute per node, every 15 s, the RSSI median and the frame rate. Reuse the constants and functions in `scripts/presence_eval/epochs.py` (`LINK_STEP_DB = 6`, rate collapse 0.7 x the node's reference rate, held-ness rules); do not reimplement them. Print one short line per 5 minutes unless something changes.
5. **LINK CHANGE.** If a held RSSI step of 6 dB or more or a held rate collapse appears on any node: stop the recording immediately, write the event (UTC, node, rssi before/after, fps before/after) to `link_change_events` and the log, tell Thaer, and wait 2 minutes. That block is invalid. Ask Thaer whether anything was touched; then start a **new block** (new recorder file) with a new `link_state_id` unless the level has demonstrably returned. If this happens in block 1 or 5, say plainly that the hour must be repeated. Never continue the same label across a change and never merge parts across it.
6. **Entry log** (`entry-log.txt`, lines `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ <who> <what>`): first line `room checked empty` only after the operator confirmed it, last line at the end of the block. Any door, corridor or entry event is written only when Thaer tells you, with the time he gives (or your clock time, marked as such). You never write an event that nobody reported.
7. When the block ends, write `block.json` per the data spec, from facts: recording base name, start/stop UTC taken from the recording itself (first and last frame time) and the operator's confirmed times, `link_state_id`, person_count, person_present, fan_on, fan_position, subject, `link_state_notes` (the RSSI you measured, plus anything Thaer reported), `link_change_events`, `entry_log`, notes. Verify the recorder's `*.summary.json` exists for every part.
8. Block summary line to chat: planned vs recorded duration (from frames), per node frames/s and RSSI start/end/median, events, files written. Then the next block.

Do not trim recordings by memory; if trimming is needed, only by the log, and say so.

## 4. Phase 2, validate, analyse, report

1. Copy the whole campaign folder to a second location outside the repo's `data/` (ask Thaer where; if he does not say, `D:\WIFI csi\phanotm\campaign-backup-<campaign_id>` after confirming the path is acceptable). Verify the copy with sha256 for every file.
2. ACTION FOR OPERATOR: photo AFTER taken (file names), any node moved? (yes/no and what), end RSSI per node can be read by you from the data.
3. `python -m scripts.presence_eval.validate_campaign <campaign folder>`. If it refuses, paste the exact refusal, say which block and which gate, **stop**, and do not run the analysis. A refusal is a valid outcome.
4. Only if the validator passes: `python -m scripts.presence_eval.run_campaign <campaign folder>`. Paste its report as written (every number is labelled measured or `[TO BE MEASURED]`). Report the C4 empty-versus-empty gate first; if C4 fails, nothing else is a result. Then C1, C1b, C2, C2b, C3, C5, C6, with C7 report-only. State plainly which candidates met or failed which criteria, using the frozen wording. If no candidate met the criteria, write "no candidate met the criteria" and stop; make no accuracy claim.
5. Both failure directions in seconds per block, as C7 requires (report-only): under-detection and ghost.
6. Full suites again with `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1 -Label campaign-post`: same six results as phase 0. Compare counts; explain any difference. Sessions guard again. `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --short` counts again; state whether anything changed since the start.

## 5. The filled checklist (a required output)

Create `reports/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/CHECKLIST-FILLED.md` mirroring every field and box of the printed sheet `Phantom-Scan-Campaign-Checklist` (sections A to D and the six-block table): header (date, operator, campaign_id, link_state_id, subject), each section-A item, the six blocks with start/stop UTC and the plan, the RSSI/fps lines, the LINK CHANGE events, the section-C file checks, and the section-D items.

For every box write one of: `[x] measured` (with the value and source file), `[x] operator-confirmed <UTC time>`, `[ ] not done`, or `[NOT PROVIDED]`. Never `[x]` without one of the first two. Add a table "values taken from data vs values from the operator". Also list every deviation from the plan with time and reason. This file is committed only if Thaer asks.

## 6. Final report (required format)

Write `reports/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/RUN-REPORT.md` and give a compact summary in chat:

## Step 3: how recording works today

| | Dashboard "Training Data Capture" | `scripts/record_dataset.py` |
|--|--|--|
| Auto-stop timer | **None.** There is only a 10 s countdown at start (`dataset-recorder-panel.tsx:17`, `:49-62`). Stopping is manual. | **Yes**, `--seconds` (`record_dataset.py:322`). |
| UDP 5005 | Not bound by the recorder. It records samples the running dashboard already ingests (`websocket.ts:2673-2675`). Start and stop come from the panel as authenticated websocket messages (`websocket.ts:465-471`, auth at `:357-359`). | **Binds 5005 itself** (`record_dataset.py:263-267`), so the dashboard would have to be stopped for the whole 3 h. |
| Files | `data/datasets/<roomId>/<roomId>__<subject>__<label>__<UTC>.jsonl`. A new part starts every 100,000 frames (`dataset-recorder.ts:55`), about every 6.4 min at 4 × 65 fps, so a 62-min block gives about 10 parts. Each part gets a `.summary.json` (`dataset-recorder.ts:209`, `:442-450`). | `<data-root>/<room>/…jsonl` (`:247`), one file plus `.summary.json` (`:307`). |
| Labels | `DATASET_LABELS` (`dataset-recorder.ts:32-40`) includes `empty`, `empty_fan`, `still`, `walking`. | Same list (`record_dataset.py:45`). |
| Demo mode | Records nothing while a demo is active (`websocket.ts:2673`). | n/a |

**My recommendation is the dashboard path.** It needs no dashboard stop or restart (rule 3), it keeps a single owner of UDP 5005, and it matches what the checklist describes: a 10 s countdown, then stopping from outside the room. There is no auto-stop, so I'll watch the frame times read-only and tell you the moment each block reaches its planned length, and you press Stop.

Neither recorder writes into the `blocks/NN_<type>/` folders the spec requires. After each block I'll **copy** the parts and summaries into the block folder, byte-identical and checked with sha256. The originals stay where the recorder wrote them, so nothing is moved or deleted.

## Claude — 2026-11-05 08:43:59Z

All three code checks are done. Nothing here needs a stop, but the trim question needs a decision from you before block 1.

**1. Room ID is only a folder name and a label.** It is used in exactly four places, all in the recorder:
- the folder name (`server/lib/dataset-recorder.ts:410`)
- the file base name (`:327`)
- the part header (`:424`)
- the status broadcast and log line (`:391`, `websocket.ts:2625`)

Starting a recording only checks the request and opens a file (`websocket.ts:2618-2632`, `dataset-recorder.ts:320-341`). It does not reset or set baselines, calibration, the detector, thresholds or tiers. The other `roomId` in `shared/schema.ts:269` is a field in the AI sidecar's advisory message, which is a separate thing and unrelated to this panel.

**2. Part boundaries can't drop a frame by design.** When a part is full, the recorder flushes the old part, opens the next one, and then writes the incoming frame into the new part, all in the same call (`dataset-recorder.ts:349-356`). I'll still measure the gap on the first part boundary of block 1, per node, against the normal frame interval, and report the number.

**3. `start_utc`/`stop_utc`: neither script trims.**
- **`validate_campaign`:** it takes the duration and the minimum from the first and last frame times of the recording (`campaign.py:141-146`). From `block.json` it only checks that start is before stop (`:118-120`). It warns, without failing, if the stated span differs from the recorded span by more than 120 s (`:147`). It also checks that entry-log lines fall within the block's start/stop ± 300 s (`:101`). So an extra walk-out of 5–15 s is accepted.
- **`run_campaign`:** it loads every frame of every part (`run_campaign.py:210-212`, via `load_tables(parts, None, None)`). Those two `None` arguments are cache paths, not a time window (`tables.py:44`). **It does not trim by `stop_utc`, so the walk-out seconds go into the analysis.**

**How big the walk-out effect is.** About 5–15 s means 3–10 windows of 1.5 s. A 6-min block has about 240 windows, so that is up to 4 %; the 11-min block has about 440, so up to 2 %. In the still block, those walk-out windows look like motion, which would make "still" look slightly better than it is. In the walking and fan-with-person blocks the person is moving anyway.

**Proposal for the trim, with no code changes during the campaign.**
1. During the campaign: you call out "walking out" as you leave, and I log the time to the second in that block's `entry-log.txt` (also written for blocks 3, 4 and 6) and in the `block.json` notes. `stop_utc` is the time you press Stop.
2. After the campaign: if you approve, I enter the walk-out as a deviation in the pre-registration's section 6 table and commit it.
3. Then I run `run_campaign` exactly as frozen, which is the pre-registered primary result.
4. Optionally, I add a small tested analysis-window option that ends each block at its logged walk-out time. Its output would be labelled **exploratory**: it can't meet or fail a criterion, and it only shows whether those seconds change anything.

Do you approve this?

**Notes for later:**
- **Calibrate Empty Room:** neither of us will press it. I'll also check that the dashboard log doesn't show it during the campaign.
- **Before block 1** I'll remind you to disable sleep and hibernate and to pause Windows updates. I can read the current power settings with `powercfg /query`, which changes nothing, if you want that check too.

I'm waiting for your step 4 answers (items 1–8).
