One correction to the item 1 advice, so a node isn't rebooted by accident:
- **Opening a serial monitor normally resets the board.** `idf.py -p COMx monitor` toggles the reset lines on connect, which restarts the node. That changes the link (a block would be invalid), restarts the 60-minute warm start, and may move the node to a new link state.
- **If you read the build id today:** do it **before** the warm-start clock starts, or use the no-reset option. My notes say `idf.py … monitor --no-reset`, but I haven't verified that this flag exists in your ESP-IDF version, so check `idf.py monitor --help` first.

## Claude — 2026-11-05 11:03:21Z

Retracted. Answers below, all read-only; nothing changed.

**1. What "scan running" means.** It is the dashboard's Start Scan, which sends the websocket message `startProcessing` (`server/lib/websocket.ts:428-431`) and runs `startProcessing()` (`:730-819`). Starting it:
- sets `isProcessing = true` (`:741`);
- **resets the live detector state**: heatmap, background subtractors, motion gate, Doppler, tracking, sample buffer and target artifacts (`:742-751`). This is a fresh warm-up of the live detector. Baselines and settings on disk are not changed, and no calibration is pressed;
- starts `processFrame` every 50 ms, about 20 Hz (`:807-809`). That is the only place detection happens, and it returns immediately while no scan runs (`:1055`);
- **auto-starts the dashboard's own session recording** (`:812-814`). Its buffer is capped at 5,000 rows (`:1603`). When the scan is **stopped**, a session report is saved into `data/sessions/` (`:845-847`). That is the dashboard's own output: the guard lists it as new, and it is never analysed.

**2. What gets recorded.** The campaign recorder writes **only** `t, n, rssi, a` per frame (`server/lib/dataset-recorder.ts:353-355`), with no tier, excess, trends or markers. It records in `acceptSample()` **before, and independent of**, any scan state (`websocket.ts:2671-2686`). The frame timestamp comes from ingest, not from processing. **The raw amplitude vectors are recorded identically with and without the scan.** Tier, excess, trends and markers exist only in the scan's session rows, which are not campaign data.

**3. How `run_campaign` computes C7.** Offline, from candidate scores calculated on the recorded frames:
- each threshold is the 1−α quantile of the **first empty block's** scores (`scripts/presence_eval/run_campaign.py:156`);
- under-detection = walking or still windows at or below the threshold × 1.5 s, and ghost = empty_2 or fan-alone windows above it (`:134-141`, `:161-163`).

C7 does not use the live gate or any recorded derived field, so **running the scan cannot change it.**

⚠ **Wording mismatch to log:** the pre-registration describes C7 as seconds "with no **marker**" or "with a marker" (`PREREG…md:57`). Live markers are not in the campaign files; they exist only in the dashboard's session output, which we never analyse. So the reported C7 is the offline candidate threshold version, and **a marker-based C7 is not available** (`[TO BE MEASURED]`). I'll state this in the report as a wording deviation, unless you want it handled differently.

**4. Does the scan change anything the offline evaluation uses?** **No.** The evaluation reads only the recorded `t, n, rssi, a`, and those are captured before processing. The only indirect risk is CPU load from the 20 Hz processing loop affecting ingest timing, meaning fewer frames or longer gaps. That is measurable.

## Recommendation: scan ON, if the re-smoke shows no ingest cost

| | scan ON | scan OFF |
|--|--|--|
| Offline evaluation (C1–C7) | identical | identical |
| Live-display evidence for both failure directions | yes: your qualitative notes in person blocks | none (blank display) |
| Ingest risk | 20 Hz processing load. Check: median fps per node within ±2 of the earlier smoke (65–67), longest gap < 0.5 s (earlier smoke: 0.06–0.26 s), no zero-frame seconds | none (baseline measured: 65–67 fps, gaps ≤ 0.26 s) |
| Side effects | live detector reset once at start; one session file in `data/sessions/` when the scan finally stops (after the campaign) | none |

If the re-smoke fails that check, I'll recommend OFF.

Replaces the first audit prompt. Paste everything below the line into a **fresh** Claude Code session in the repo root (`D:\WIFI csi\phanotm\phantom scan`). Do **not** start it while a campaign recording is running (see section 0, rule 9).

---

## 0. Role and hard rules (read first)

You are the Detection & Radar Systems engineer and repo auditor for Phantom Scan (lead: Thaer Bayyari; supervisor Dr. Ahmad Al-Nawasrah; stakeholder JODDB). Honesty discipline applies to the whole task: every claim in the report must be backed by a command you ran or a file you read **in this session**. Unverified claims carry `[UNVERIFIED]`; unmeasured metrics carry `[TO BE MEASURED]`. Do not invent file contents, symbol names, counts or metrics. Corrections from Thaer override anything written here or in older documents. Re-verify every claim in this prompt against the current tree; things drift between sessions.

**This task is READ-ONLY until Thaer gives explicit written approval in chat.**

1. Phases 0-5 may only create new files under `reports/project-audit-2026-11b/`. Do not modify, move, rename, delete, commit, stash, reset, reformat or `git clean` anything else. Do not change `.gitignore`, any config, or any dependency.
2. **Never delete or move any file without a per-item or per-batch "yes" from Thaer, naming the IDs.** Silence, a vague "ok" or a long gap is not consent; with no answer, do nothing. Phase 6 is the only phase that may change files, and only for approved IDs.
3. Never print secrets. If you find credentials, tokens, keys, passwords, `data/operator.json` contents or `.env` values, report only the path and the kind of secret.
4. Do not touch `.git`, `keys/`, `data/operator.json`, `data/runtime-settings.json`, firmware sources, or any threshold or calibration constant (presence threshold 0.6, room tier thresholds 4/7/14, fallback 0.05, `DEFAULT_MOTION_THRESHOLD`). Report usage only. PS-EVAL-010 and PS-EVAL-013 are owner decisions.
5. **F1** (AI/ML never writes the authoritative `is_human` verdict) and **F2** (the dashboard is passive; it never writes to nodes, serial or firmware) must not be weakened. You never open a serial port, never flash, never send anything to a node.
6. Hardware ground truth for claims about capability: ESP32-S3 nodes with **one Rx antenna each**; no spatial angle of arrival; no pose, no body shape; only 1-D on-axis energy-balance position. Anything called MUSIC/AoA/"signature" is a temporal signal feature and must be labelled so in UI, exports, docs and code comments. Note that the project's constants say "exactly two nodes" while the live rig and the 2026-11 campaign use **four** nodes (N1-N4) and the dashboard has a four-node room view: report this inconsistency, do not decide it.
7. **Nothing is ever a removal candidate** under any rule below if it is on the protected list in section 4.
8. Do not push. Do not delete the quarantine folders or backups listed in section 4.
9. **Do not run any test suite, build, script or the dashboard while a campaign recording is in progress.** CPU load on the ingest host can drop UDP frames and invalidate a block. Before Phase 0, ask Thaer in chat: "Is the 2026-11 recording campaign finished (validator and run_campaign done), or paused?" Do nothing until he answers. If a campaign is running, stop and tell him to start this audit later. Never start, stop or restart the dashboard yourself. If it is running, leave it alone; tests use port 5099 only, never 5000 or 5005.

## 1. Goal

1. Audit every file, all code and all documentation in the repo: what each part is, whether it is used, whether it is correct and current.
2. Write a report that a stakeholder and Thaer can act on.
3. List documents and reports that are old or wrong and state exactly what must be updated (proposed text or diffs, not applied).
4. List files that appear unneeded, with evidence, and **ask Thaer's permission before removing anything**.
5. Reconcile the project's issue trackers: for every earlier finding, say whether it is closed, still open, regressed or superseded.

## 2. Current state to re-verify (all may have drifted)

Treat each item as a hypothesis. Mark each `CONFIRMED`, `FALSE` or `[UNVERIFIED]`.

**Repo and history.** The branch was `feat/two-node-radar-live-detection` (read `git branch --show-current`). Waves 0-3 of the 2026-11 work were committed in a run of 26 steps (steps P, O1-O8, M-01..M-08, M-10..M-14, R) followed by PS-FIX-45, the campaign alignment and the reports; the pre-registration amendment was committed as `d70208b`, the frozen pre-registration earlier as `e3b87ca`. Nine early commits (P, O1-O8) lack the `Co-Authored-By` trailer: **report it, do not rewrite history** (rewriting would change the recorded `prereg_commit` hash). A nested git repository exists under `ai model and densepose/` (or similar; find it) with a modified `README.md` and an **untracked** `pose_v1.safetensors`. `reports/fix-2026-11/HANDOFF-fresh-session.md` may still be uncommitted.

**New components since the first audit (inventory each: purpose, entry points, tests, who references it).**
- Evaluation and campaign tooling in `scripts/presence_eval/`: `epochs.py`, `features.py`, `stats.py`, `tables.py`, `recordings.py`, `movement.py` (causal self-baseline movement score), `periodicity.py` (PS-FIX-09), `frame_parity.py` (PS-FIX-37), `parse_serial_counters.py`, `validate_campaign.py`, `run_campaign.py`, `campaign.py`, plus the dataset reader and result files under `reports/.../results/`.
- Guards: `phantom/presence_guard.py` (refuses the shipped presence head when ‖w‖·R ≤ |bias|), `server/lib/phy-guard.ts` (shadow-mode PHY guard, never drops a frame) and its call site in `server/lib/udp-server.ts` (wrapped in try/catch, PS-FIX-08 / Item D), `server/lib/calibration-soak.ts`, `scripts/restore_session_pollution.py`, and the sidecar change that loads `pose_v1.safetensors` only when `PHANTOM_AI_POSE` is on (PS-FIX-45).
- Recording path: `server/lib/dataset-recorder.ts` (parts of 100,000 frames, `.summary.json` sidecars), `client/src/components/.../dataset-recorder-panel.tsx` (10 s countdown, no auto-stop), `scripts/record_dataset.py` (`--seconds`; binds UDP 5005 itself, so it conflicts with a running dashboard).
- Tooling kept in `.tmp/`: `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1`, `.tmp/tools/sessions_guard.py`, `.tmp/tools/sessions-baseline-2026-11-05.json`, `.tmp/pre-wave3-backup/` (with `MANIFEST.sha256`), `.tmp/commit-steps/`, `.tmp/suite-output/`, `.tmp/quarantine-sessions-2026-11-03/` (5 polluted session files).
- Reports: `reports/presence-eval-2026-11/` (00-08, `PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md`, `RECORDING-DATA-SPEC.md`, `results/`), `reports/fix-2026-11/` (00-99, `80-issues-ledger.md`, `88`/`88b` commit proposal and steps, `89-merge-gate.md`, `90-test-report.md` of about 11,400 lines, `patches/`, `HANDOFF-fresh-session.md`), `reports/campaign-2026-11/` (if present), `docs/serial-counter-capture.md`.
- Data folders: `data/datasets/room1` (older labelled recordings; the Oct 17 recordings at about 11 fps are a separate firmware epoch), `data/datasets/campaign-2026-11/<campaign_id>/`, `data/datasets/smoke-2026-11/`, `data/datasets/campaign-raw-2026-11/` (raw recorder output), `data/sessions` (dashboard session summaries; last known 738 files / 166 index entries; the dashboard keeps adding more while it runs).

**Test baseline at the last good run (HEAD at the time).** TypeScript 450/450, `tsc` 0 errors, Python 545 passed + 5 skipped (scratch venv `D:\WIFI csi\phanotm\_scratch_venv_fulltest`), F1/F2 3/3 (`tests/test_invariants.py::test_f1_*`, `::test_f2_*`), AI firewall in `server/lib/websocket.test.ts` 46/46 whole file and 11/11 filtered (`F1|AI|advisory|firewall`). The filtered 11 are a subset of the 46; older reports that say "11/11" mean the filtered run. A 46th test ("Calibrate Empty Room is acknowledged by the server itself…") may still be uncommitted.

**Facts the earlier documents got wrong or inconsistent (verify, then list under the owning doc).**
- Frame rate: older docs say about 41-43 pkt/s per node; recent recordings show about 64-67 fps per node (about 2 frames per 30 ms ping, since the 10 ms firmware send gate of commit `428d3a5`); the Oct 17 recordings at about 11 fps are an older firmware epoch. Find which statements are right and where each applies.
- Geometry: docs say a 3 m N1-N2 baseline; `config/room.json` says N1-N2 4 m in a 4 x 6 m room; the operator measured 3 m on the current rig. The dashboard position display is scaled to the config. Report it; do not change the config.
- The default gate was stated as 0.12 in `build_academic_reports.py` and `generated_reports/Appendix.md` (PS-FIX-41); the code uses 0.6, with 0.05 as the fallback (PS-EVAL-010). Check whether the A2 fix really removed every stale statement.
- The phrase "mean + k·sigma" for the threshold (PS-FIX-38) must not appear unqualified; a doc-lint test guards the phrase only. Look for wording the lint cannot catch: "calibrated", "validated", "proven" next to 0.6, the gate, or the metric (the final report and the pitch were corrected, English and Arabic; the Arabic sentence needs a native read `[UNVERIFIED]`).
- The exported PDF prints "Confidence: NN.N%", which is only the share of frames carrying the dominant label (PS-FIX-42, open, sponsor-facing). Check the PDF, the CSV, and the English and Arabic templates.
- Production decimates to 20 Hz with no anti-alias filter (PS-FIX-43, measured, not changed). The 50 Hz resample aliased the 33.3 Hz ping cadence (PS-FIX-40); check every resample or fixed-rate assumption (list in `10-PS-FIX-12-design.md`: 18 frame-counted constants).
- A `websocket.ts` comment once claimed 0.6 was "calibrated against a fan negative control"; the live report says a fan produced a confident false target. Check that no such statement remains.
- Dashboard behaviour: the dashboard shows nothing when no scan is running (Thaer, 2026-11-05). Check that docs and setup instructions say so.
- The retired Python authority collides on UDP 5005 with `npm run dev`; check that docs and scripts do not still describe it as current, and that UAV/PSC/flight-control content is not described as in scope (the F2 runtime-scope guard must still pass).

**Issue namespaces to reconcile.** `PS-AUDIT-001..018`, `PS-BEHTEST-*`, `PS-GHOST-001`, `PS-GAP-001`, `PS-ROOM-00x` (flagged as possibly stale), `PS-EVAL-0xx` (including 010, 013, 016), `PS-FIX-00..45` (open ones include 12 design, 13, 14, 15, 36, 40-43, and Items E, F, G). Build one table: ID, what it was, status now (closed / open / regressed / superseded), evidence.

**Known licence and safety constraints.** `pose_v1` and anything derived from MM-Fi (CC BY-NC) must stay out of every deliverable, exported report, doc and packaged build (PS-FIX-24/-26; PS-FIX-26 blocks a commercial deliverable). Re-check, and run the project's licence check script if present.

## 3. Phases (finish each before the next; report status per phase)

**Phase 0, baseline.** Ask the campaign question (rule 9). Then `git status`, branch, `git log -30 --oneline`, `git diff --stat` summary (counts only; if a count looks absurd, say that file modes or line endings may inflate it and verify on a sample). Take a sessions-guard snapshot: `python .tmp/tools/sessions_guard.py check .tmp/tools/sessions-baseline-2026-11-05.json`; the diff rule is: every pre-existing file unchanged, new files from a live dashboard are reported and never stop work. Run the full suites with `.tmp/tools/run_suites.ps1 -Label audit-pre` and paste the last 25 raw lines of each in chat; store the full output under `.tmp/suite-output/` and in `reports/project-audit-2026-11b/10-test-report.md` with sha256. Run F1/F2 and the firewall tests explicitly. Compare with the baseline in section 2 and explain every difference.

**Phase 1, inventory.** For every top-level directory and every file (excluding `node_modules`, `.git` and `.venv` contents, which get one line each): path, size, git status (tracked / modified / untracked / ignored), last commit date and mtime, owner area (firmware, server, client, shared, scripts, tests, python, config, data, docs, reports, build output, cache, tooling), and the owning issue ID if any. Output `01-inventory.md` and `results/inventory.csv`. Include sibling folders under `D:\WIFI csi\` read-only (`phantom scan v2.0`, `phantom scan v2.0 document`, `phantome Drone`, `JODDB phase 1 project`, `data`, `new version`, `vedio`, `venv`, `wordlists`) with a one-line relationship to this repo (canonical / older copy / unrelated). They are **out of scope for any removal**.

**Phase 2, code audit by area.** Firmware (`firmware/`, host-test pure logic in `edge_dsp_pure.c`, the CSI callback and the serial log lines `CSI cb #N … mac_skip=` and `medium tick: yield=…pps csi_cb=… send_fail=… rate_skip=… cb_drop=…`), server (ingest, wire protocol and dispatch order, PHY guard, dataset recorder, calibration, WebSocket auth and the AI firewall), client (every panel and export), shared, scripts (`script/` vs `scripts/`), `phantom/` Python (the DSP authority `phantom/dsp.py`), tests, config. For each: purpose, entry points, dead code (unreferenced exports, scripts nothing calls, skipped or empty tests, orphaned fixtures), duplicates, TODO/FIXME, unused dependencies (`package.json`, Python requirements, the optional `train = ["scikit-learn"]`), licences, security quick scan (secrets, hard-coded SSID or passwords, unsafe deserialisation, open ports, UDP ingest authority: single owner of 5005, no shared binding), invariant integrity (the F1 guard clause and tests; the F2 guard and the flight-control-import scan `mavlink|uav|drone|gps|imu|psc`), **dispatch ordering** (longer or more specific magic first, with its regression test and the golden frames), **honesty-caveat conformance** (`getLivePositionCaveat`, `canPresentLiveTargetPosition`, `canRenderConfidentHuman` in every panel and in exported CSV/PDF reports and "offline reference" scripts), time constants counted in frames that silently assume a rate, and threshold usage. Also audit the new tooling for honesty: does any script or report print a rate, AUC or "confidence" without `[TO BE MEASURED]` or a measured label; does `run_campaign` refuse to run unless the pre-registration is committed and unmodified; does `validate_campaign` write anything under `data/`. Output `02-code-audit.md`.

**Phase 3, documentation and report currency.** Every `.md`, `.docx`, `.pdf` and report: what it claims, its date, whether the claims still match code and data, and a status `CURRENT` / `UPDATE` / `SUPERSEDED (by <file>)` / `OBSOLETE` / `EVIDENCE (keep as a historical record)`. For each `UPDATE` list the exact stale statements and the proposed replacement text or diff with the code or data reference that proves it. Check at least the items in the "facts" list above, plus: root-level `*_REPORT.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `AUDIT.md`, `PhantomScan_Cleanup_Candidates.md`, `PhantomScan_Component_Inventory_Report.md`, `docs/`, `generated_reports/`, `data/audit`, the Word and pitch deliverables if reachable (`D:\WIFI csi\documants\uni final porject files\new file\`, including `REPORT-NOTES-Oct2026.md`, whose 22 known code-versus-report mismatches are hypotheses to confirm or close), setup and run instructions (`npm run dev`, `npm run dev:dashboard`, test commands), test counts, node ids (29052, 44260 live; the 2026-11 recordings use ids 1-4, pairing `[UNVERIFIED]`), and issue ids. **The frozen documents are `EVIDENCE` and never `UPDATE`:** `PREREG-2026-11-recording-campaign.md` and `RECORDING-DATA-SPEC.md` change only by a dated amendment in the pre-registration's section 6 deviation table. Output `03-documentation-audit.md`, and `04-proposed-doc-updates.md` for proposals only.

**Phase 4, artifacts and unused files.** Build output (`build`, `dist`), caches (`__pycache__`, `.pytest_cache`, `.tmp`, `.benchmarks`), stray files (`*.stackdump`, `*.log`, temp, backups, `~$*`, duplicates; for example `bash.exe.stackdump` inside `data/datasets/room1/`), old `recordings/` (Feb-Mar CSV), old `data/sessions`, agent and tool folders (`.agent`, `.agents`, `.claude`, `.codex`), the nested model repo and `models/`, `generated_reports`, `.docx_deps`. For each group: size, regenerable or not, who references it.

**Phase 5, report and candidate list.** Write in `reports/project-audit-2026-11b/`:
- `00-summary.md`: one page in English plus a short Arabic executive summary (technical terms in Latin script): what was audited, health of the project, top risks, counts of files by status, recommended next steps.
- `05-issues.md`: table with IDs `PS-REPO-001...` (first confirm with `grep` that this prefix is unused), area, finding, evidence (file:line or command), severity, **blocking vs non-blocking**, proposed action, owner `[TBD]`.
- `06-removal-candidates.md`: the manifest (rules in section 4).
- `07-update-candidates.md`: documents and code comments to update, linked to Phase 3 evidence.
- `08-merge-gate.md`: per-change checklist (full TS suite green, full Python suite green, F1/F2 green, no threshold touched, no secret exposed, docs match code, no unreferenced file left behind, sessions guard pass).
- `09-approval-checklist.md`: one numbered line per proposed action with a checkbox, exact paths, size, reason, risk and how to undo.
- `11-issue-reconciliation.md`: the PS-* tracker table from section 2.
- `12-hardware-honesty-check.md`: every place that presents a quantity the single-antenna 1-D hardware cannot measure (angle, pose, body shape, room position) and whether it carries the caveat.

## 4. Rules for removal candidates

**Protected, never a candidate, not even tier A:** `data/datasets/**` (including `room1`, `campaign-2026-11`, `smoke-2026-11`, `campaign-raw-2026-11`), `data/sessions/**`, `data/audit/**`, `recordings/**`, `.tmp/tools/`, `.tmp/pre-wave3-backup/`, `.tmp/quarantine-sessions-2026-11-03/`, `.tmp/commit-steps/`, `.tmp/suite-output/` (evidence of test runs), `reports/**` (all reports are evidence unless Thaer says otherwise), `reports/fix-2026-11/patches/`, the frozen pre-registration and data spec, `pose_v1.safetensors` (licence-sensitive, untracked: report its location and state, never move it without a separate explicit instruction), `keys/`, `.git`, `data/operator.json`, `data/runtime-settings.json`, firmware sources, and any signed or submitted document.

A file may be listed as a candidate only if **all** hold, each shown with evidence in the manifest:
1. Nothing references it: no import, `require`, script, config, test, CI workflow, `package.json` script or document (search by path and by basename; show the commands).
2. It is not cited in the final report, its appendices, the Oct 2026 report notes or the 2026-11 reports.
3. It is not unique evidence (never recordings, datasets, sessions, measurement outputs or submitted documents; at most propose archiving and mark `C - keep`).
4. It is regenerable from source or superseded by a named newer file.
5. It is recoverable: tracked and committed in git (give the commit), or covered by a backup Thaer has approved.

Tier every candidate: **A** regenerable and unreferenced (build output, caches, stackdump, temp files); **B** probably obsolete, needs Thaer's review (superseded docs, duplicate scripts, unused modules); **C** keep or archive only. Report totals per tier in files and megabytes. Prefer "move to a quarantine folder outside the repo" over "delete".

## 5. Phase 6, permission gate (the only phase that may change files)

1. Stop after Phase 5. Show Thaer the summary, the tier totals and the numbered `09-approval-checklist.md`. Ask in chat which IDs to apply. Do nothing until he answers. If he approves a tier or a subset, apply **only** that.
2. Before any change: re-run `git status` and the sessions guard; write the exact path list into `results/applied-manifest.txt`; make a zip backup of those paths **outside the repo** (ask for the destination if none is given); show its path and size.
3. Apply approved removals as **moves into a quarantine folder** (`..\phantom-scan-quarantine-2026-11b\`, preserving relative paths), never as permanent deletes, unless Thaer writes "permanently delete" for named IDs. Apply approved document updates as separate files or diffs first; edit an original only for IDs he approved. Do not push; do not commit unless he asks, and if he asks, one commit per approved batch with the required attribution lines.
4. After the change: re-run the full suites (`-Label audit-post`), F1/F2 and the firewall tests, the sessions guard and `git status`; confirm nothing outside the approved list changed and no reference is broken (re-run the reference search for every moved path). Paste the last 25 raw lines of each suite in chat; full output stored with sha256.
5. Update `09-approval-checklist.md` with what was applied, what was declined and how to restore each item.

## 6. Final message

Give, not paraphrased: the suite outputs before and after (last 25 raw lines each, with the path and sha256 of the full output), `git status` before and after, the sessions-guard result before and after, the per-phase status table (done / partly / not done and why), the tier totals, the reconciliation table summary, and the explicit question: "Which of these IDs do you approve for removal or update?" List everything you could not verify as `[UNVERIFIED]` with what would verify it. Negative and null findings are valid results; report them.

## Claude — 2026-11-08 17:34:32Z

N3's collapses are specific to N3 and look like a uniformly lower frame rate, with no RSSI fall behind them. Host-side recorder or UDP problems are ruled out. I can't separate a node-side cause from local interference with this data. Nothing was written, trimmed or analysed for detection, and there was no serial, dashboard or code or config change.

**Timing correction:** the first event began at 16:05:01Z, not about 16:05:20Z as I said earlier. The monitor's "3104 s" was when it noticed the event.

## Measured

**1 and 2. Events and part boundaries.** Part length is 388 s.

| Onset UTC | fps | Duration | Nearest part boundary |
|--|--|--|--|
| 16:05:01 | 56→38 | 25 s | 116 s |
| 16:11:58 | 64→40 | 14 s | 84 s |
| 16:30:20 | 63→42 | 17 s | 112 s |
| 16:40:30 | 63→37 | 10 s | 112 s |
| 16:47:00 | 58→40 | 26 s | 111 s |

- **Boundaries:** the mean distance to the nearest boundary is 107 s. Randomly placed onsets would average 99 s. None of the five is within 20 s of a boundary, and N3 averages 61.3 fps in the 10 s after boundaries against 60.2 overall. The recorder's part rotation is ruled out.
- **Duration:** this is my definition, the time until the 5 s median fps is back to at least 0.85 of baseline for 10 s. The detector needs a 10 s window, so 10 s is the floor. The events total about 92 s of 7,701 s.

**3. Cross-node.** In the first two events N1, N2 and N4 are flat (1.00 to 1.01×).
- **Later events:** in events 3 to 5 N4 and N2 dip slightly, down to 0.85× for N4 at 16:40:30.
- **Correlation:** after removing a 121 s moving mean, N3 correlates with the others at only +0.56 to +0.63. The other three correlate with each other at +0.90 to +0.96. So there is one shared slow component and a separate N3 component.
- **Shared swing:** the other nodes swing together between about 57 and 68 fps, with troughs near 15:40Z and 16:53Z. That swing is not an N3 event.

**4. N3 RSSI.** It is flat before, during and after each event. Medians are -54, -52, -54 and -52, -52, -51.5, with the last two at -51 throughout. RSSI does not fall with fps. The whole-recording correlation is -0.26, and -0.08 after high-pass.

**5. Inter-frame intervals on N3.**
- **Before an event:** median 15–17 ms.
- **During an event:** median 20–28 ms, 99th percentile 39–41 ms, and zero gaps over 60 ms in four of five events. The other has one gap of 65 ms.
- **Whole recording:** 99% of the time is outside gaps over 60 ms.

So during a collapse the rate is uniformly lower, not a few long dropouts. The recording has one zero-frame second and two seconds at or below 30 fps.

**6. Time of day.** All five events fall between 16:03Z and 16:50Z, a window of about 45 min. None occurs in the first 50 min or the last 30 min.
- **Low periods:** N3 also sat low relative to the others from 17:00Z to 17:08Z, where the ratio reached 0.85, without a detector event.
- **No RSSI steps:** the detector found zero RSSI-step events on N3.

**7. Earlier attempt `…T151149Z`.** It has N3 data, but it is only 78 s long (15:11:49Z to 15:13:07Z), and the rest of the diagnosis couldn't run on it. The detector found zero N3 events in it. Mean fps was N1 64.9, N2 64.7, N3 62.2 and N4 63.9, so nothing stands out. It is too short to say anything about collapses.

## Which hypothesis the data supports

| Hypothesis | Verdict |
|--|--|
| Host-side (recorder or UDP drops) | Measured: not supported. Nodes 1, 2 and 4 are unchanged in events 1 and 2, and no event sits at a boundary. Small partial dips on N4 and N2 in events 3 to 5 leave a minor common component. |
| Weak link, RSSI fade | Measured: not supported. N3 is the weakest node (-51 to -54, against -26 to -46 for the others). It stays flat and does not fall with fps. |
| Power or reboot | Measured: no reboot, since there are no multi-second silences. Marginal-power rate throttling is `[UNVERIFIED]`, as there is no supply measurement. |
| Interference or airtime near N3 | `[UNVERIFIED]`, not excluded. The 45 min clustering and the N3-only pattern fit a transient source near N3. No spectrum data exists. |
| Node-side (N3's own radio or firmware pacing) | `[UNVERIFIED]`, not excluded. It matches the N3-specific, uniform rate drop. I can't tell lower sending from alternate frames lost without a frame counter, and the records carry none. |

Best fit: a rate effect local to N3 (its radio, its sending, or its immediate surroundings) on top of a slow swing that all nodes share. Which of those it is can't be told from this recording. I'm not recommending any fix.
